37	## 1. Detect Input State
38	
39	```bash
40	VALIDATION_FILE=$(ls "${PHASE_DIR}"/*-VALIDATION.md 2>/dev/null | head -1)
41	SUMMARY_FILES=$(ls "${PHASE_DIR}"/*-SUMMARY.md 2>/dev/null)
42	```
43	
44	- **State A** (`VALIDATION_FILE` non-empty): Audit existing
45	- **State B** (`VALIDATION_FILE` empty, `SUMMARY_FILES` non-empty): Reconstruct from artifacts
46	- **State C** (`SUMMARY_FILES` empty): Exit — "Phase {N} not executed. Run /gsd-execute-phase {N} ${GSD_WS} first."
47	
48	## 2. Discovery
49	
50	### 2a. Read Phase Artifacts
51	
52	Read all PLAN and SUMMARY files. Extract: task lists, requirement IDs, key-files changed, verify blocks.
53	
54	### 2b. Build Requirement-to-Task Map
55	
56	Per task: `{ task_id, plan_id, wave, requirement_ids, has_automated_command }`
57	
58	### 2c. Detect Test Infrastructure
59	
60	State A: Parse from existing VALIDATION.md Test Infrastructure table.
61	State B: Filesystem scan:
62	
63	```bash
64	find . -name "pytest.ini" -o -name "jest.config.*" -o -name "vitest.config.*" -o -name "pyproject.toml" 2>/dev/null | head -10
65	find . \( -name "*.test.*" -o -name "*.spec.*" -o -name "test_*" \) -not -path "*/node_modules/*" 2>/dev/null | head -40
66	```
67	
68	### 2d. Cross-Reference
69	
70	Match each requirement to existing tests by filename, imports, test descriptions. Record: requirement → test_file → status.
71	
72	## 3. Gap Analysis
73	
74	Classify each requirement:
75	
76	| Status  | Criteria                                  |
77	| ------- | ----------------------------------------- |
78	| COVERED | Test exists, targets behavior, runs green |
79	| PARTIAL | Test exists, failing or incomplete        |
80	| MISSING | No test found                             |
81	
82	Build: `{ task_id, requirement, gap_type, suggested_test_path, suggested_command }`
83	
84	No gaps → skip to Step 6, set `nyquist_compliant: true`.
85	
86	## 4. Present Gap Plan
87	
88	**Text mode (`workflow.text_mode: true` in config or `--text` flag):** Set `TEXT_MODE=true` if `--text` is present in `$ARGUMENTS` OR `text_mode` from init JSON is `true`. When TEXT_MODE is active, replace every `AskUserQuestion` call with a plain-text numbered list and ask the user to type their choice number. This is required for non-Claude runtimes (OpenAI Codex, Gemini CLI, etc.) where `AskUserQuestion` is not available.
89	Call AskUserQuestion with gap table and options:
90	
91	1. "Fix all gaps" → Step 5
92	2. "Skip — mark manual-only" → add to Manual-Only, Step 6
93	3. "Cancel" → exit
94	
95	## 5. Spawn gsd-nyquist-auditor
96	
97	Print: `◆ Spawning nyquist auditor... (runs in a subagent — no output until it returns, ~1–5 min; expected, not a freeze)`
98	
99	```
100	Agent(
101	  prompt="Read /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/agents/gsd-nyquist-auditor.md for instructions.\n\n" +
102	    "<files_to_read>{PLAN, SUMMARY, impl files, VALIDATION.md}</files_to_read>" +
103	    "<gaps>{gap list}</gaps>" +
104	    "<test_infrastructure>{framework, config, commands}</test_infrastructure>" +
105	    "<constraints>Never modify impl files. Max 3 debug iterations. Escalate impl bugs.</constraints>" +
106	    "${AGENT_SKILLS_AUDITOR}",
107	  subagent_type="gsd-nyquist-auditor",
108	  model="{AUDITOR_MODEL}",
109	  description="Fill validation gaps for Phase {N}"
110	)
111	```
112	
113	> **ORCHESTRATOR RULE — CODEX RUNTIME**: After calling Agent() above, stop working on this task immediately. Do not read more files, edit code, or run tests related to this task while the subagent is active. Wait for the subagent to return its result. This prevents duplicate work, conflicting edits, and wasted context. Only resume when the subagent result is available.
114	
115	Handle return:
116	
117	- `## GAPS FILLED` → record tests + map updates, Step 6
118	- `## PARTIAL` → record resolved, move escalated to manual-only, Step 6
119	- `## ESCALATE` → move all to manual-only, Step 6
120	
121	## 6. Generate/Update VALIDATION.md
122	
123	**State B (create):**
124	
125	1. Read template from `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/gsd-core/templates/VALIDATION.md`
126	2. Fill: frontmatter, Test Infrastructure, Per-Task Map, Manual-Only, Sign-Off
127	3. Write to `${PHASE_DIR}/${PADDED_PHASE}-VALIDATION.md`
128	
129	**State A (update):**

132	2. Append audit trail:

138	| ---------- | ----- |
139	| Gaps found | {N}   |
140	| Resolved   | {M}   |
141	| Escalated  | {K}   |
142	```
143	
144	## 7. Commit

147	git add {test_files}
148	git commit -m "test(phase-${PHASE}): add Nyquist validation tests"
149	
150	gsd_run query commit "docs(phase-${PHASE}): add/update validation strategy"
151	```

153	## 8. Results + Routing

167	## Guidelines
168	
169	**Status values:**
170	
171	- `passed` — All must-haves verified, no blockers
172	- `gaps_found` — One or more critical gaps found
173	- `human_needed` — Automated checks pass but human verification required
174	
175	**Evidence types:**
176	
177	- For EXISTS: "File at path, exports X"
178	- For SUBSTANTIVE: "N lines, has patterns X, Y, Z"
179	- For WIRED: "Line N: code that connects A to B"
180	- For FAILED: "Missing because X" or "Stub because Y"
181	
182	**Severity levels:**
183	
184	- 🛑 Blocker: Prevents goal achievement, must fix
185	- ⚠️ Warning: Indicates incomplete but doesn't block
186	- ℹ️ Info: Notable but not problematic
187	
188	**Fix plan generation:**
189	
190	- Only generate if gaps_found
191	- Group related fixes into single plans
192	- Keep to 2-3 tasks per plan
193	- Include verification task in each plan
194	
195	---

```
25	| 2  | Windows Explorer operations do not hang during background metadata refresh | VERIFIED (automated portion) | `drain_file_pointer_completions()` called in `handle_open` (line 111), `handle_read` (line 245), and `handle_read_directory` (line 32) — all WinFsp entry points drain the channel before doing real work, so resolved metadata is applied eagerly. |

The poll loop in `handle_read` bounds individual read waits to 5s (`Duration::from_secs(5)`, line 291). |
27	| 4  | Windows desktop E2E tests pass with the async resolution path | VERIFIED | Runtime tested on Windows 11 + WinFsp: 9/9 FUSE E2E tests passed, 24/24 unit tests passed, desktop app mounted and operated without hangs. |
```

93	3. Extract `gaps` (items that failed)

153	2. **Merge PLAN frontmatter truths** from Step 2b (these add plan-specific detail)
154	3. **Deduplicate:** If a PLAN truth clearly restates a roadmap SC, keep the roadmap SC wording (it's the contract)
155	4. **If neither 2a nor 2b produced any truths**, fall back to Option C below
156	
157	**CRITICAL:** PLAN frontmatter must-haves must NOT reduce scope. If ROADMAP.md defines 5 Success Criteria but the plan only lists 3 in must_haves, all 5 must still be verified. The plan can ADD must-haves but never subtract roadmap SCs.

164	2. **Derive truths:** "What must be TRUE?" — list 3-7 observable, testable behaviors
165	3. **Derive artifacts:** For each truth, "What must EXIST?" — map to concrete file paths
166	4. **Derive key links:** For each artifact, "What must be CONNECTED?" — this is where stubs hide
167	5. **Document derived must-haves** before proceeding

169	## Step 3: Verify Observable Truths
170	
171	For each truth, determine if codebase enables it.
172	
173	**Verification status:**
174	
175	- ✓ VERIFIED: All supporting artifacts pass all checks
176	- ✗ FAILED: One or more artifacts missing, stub, or unwired
177	- ? UNCERTAIN: Can't verify programmatically (needs human)
178	
179	For each truth:
180	
181	1. Identify supporting artifacts
182	2. Check artifact status (Step 4)
183	3. Check wiring status (Step 5)
184	4. **Before marking FAIL:** Check for override (Step 3b)
185	5. Determine truth status

187	## Step 3b: Check Verification Overrides
188	
189	Before marking any must-have as FAILED, check the VERIFICATION.md frontmatter for an `overrides:` entry that matches this must-have.
190	
191	**Override check procedure:**
192	
193	1. Parse `overrides:` array from VERIFICATION.md frontmatter (if present)
194	2. For each override entry, normalize both the override `must_have` and the current truth to lowercase, strip punctuation, collapse whitespace
195	3. Split into tokens and compute intersection — match if 80% token overlap in either direction
196	4. Key technical terms (file paths, component names, API endpoints) have higher weight
197	
198	**If override found:**
199	
200	- Mark as `PASSED (override)` instead of FAIL
201	- Evidence: `Override: {reason} — accepted by {accepted_by} on {accepted_at}`
202	- Count toward passing score, not failing score
203	
204	**If no override found:**
205	
206	- Mark as FAILED as normal
207	- Consider suggesting an override if the failure looks intentional (alternative implementation exists)
208	
209	**Suggesting overrides:** When a must-have FAILs but evidence shows an alternative implementation that achieves the same intent, include an override suggestion in the report:
210	
211	````markdown
212	**This looks intentional.** To accept this deviation, add to VERIFICATION.md frontmatter:
213	
214	```yaml
215	overrides:
216	  - must_have: '{must-have text}'
217	    reason: '{why this deviation is acceptable}'
218	    accepted_by: '{name}'
219	    accepted_at: '{ISO timestamp}'
220	```
221	````

225	## Step 4: Verify Artifacts (Three Levels)
226	
227	Use `gsd-tools query` for artifact verification against must_haves in PLAN frontmatter:
228	
229	```bash
230	ARTIFACT_RESULT=$(gsd-tools query verify.artifacts "$PLAN_PATH")
231	````
232	
233	Parse JSON result: `{ all_passed, passed, total, artifacts: [{path, exists, issues, passed}] }`
234	
235	For each artifact in result:
236	
237	- `exists=false` → MISSING
238	- `issues` contains "Only N lines" or "Missing pattern" → STUB
239	- `passed=true` → VERIFIED
240	
241	**Artifact status mapping:**
242	
243	| exists | issues empty | Status     |
244	| ------ | ------------ | ---------- |
245	| true   | true         | ✓ VERIFIED |
246	| true   | false        | ✗ STUB     |
247	| false  | -            | ✗ MISSING  |
248	
249	**For wiring verification (Level 3)**, check imports/usage manually for artifacts that pass Levels 1-2:
250	
251	```bash
252	# Import check
253	grep -r "import.*$artifact_name" "${search_path:-src/}" --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" 2>/dev/null | wc -l
254	
255	# Usage check (beyond imports)
256	grep -r "$artifact_name" "${search_path:-src/}" --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" 2>/dev/null | grep -v "import" | wc -l
257	```
258	
259	**Wiring status:**
260	
261	- WIRED: Imported AND used
262	- ORPHANED: Exists but not imported/used
263	- PARTIAL: Imported but not used (or vice versa)
264	
265	### Final Artifact Status
266	
267	| Exists | Substantive | Wired | Status      |
268	| ------ | ----------- | ----- | ----------- |
269	| ✓      | ✓           | ✓     | ✓ VERIFIED  |
270	| ✓      | ✓           | ✗     | ⚠️ ORPHANED |
271	| ✓      | ✗           | -     | ✗ STUB      |
272	| ✗      | -           | -     | ✗ MISSING   |
273	
274	## Step 4b: Data-Flow Trace (Level 4)
275	
276	Artifacts that pass Levels 1-3 (exist, substantive, wired) can still be hollow if their data source produces empty or hardcoded values. Level 4 traces upstream from the artifact to verify real data flows through the wiring.
277	
278	**When to run:** For each artifact that passes Level 3 (WIRED) and renders dynamic data (components, pages, dashboards — not utilities or configs).
279	
280	**How:**
281	
282	1. **Identify the data variable** — what state/prop does the artifact render?
283	
284	```bash
285	# Find state variables that are rendered in JSX/TSX
286	grep -n -E "useState|useQuery|useSWR|useStore|props\." "$artifact" 2>/dev/null
287	```
288	
289	2. **Trace the data source** — where does that variable get populated?
290	
291	```bash
292	# Find the fetch/query that populates the state
293	grep -n -A 5 "set${STATE_VAR}\|${STATE_VAR}\s*=" "$artifact" 2>/dev/null | grep -E "fetch|axios|query|store|dispatch|props\."
294	```
295	
296	3. **Verify the source produces real data** — does the API/store return actual data or static/empty values?
297	
298	```bash
299	# Check the API route or data source for real DB queries vs static returns
300	grep -n -E "prisma\.|db\.|query\(|findMany|findOne|select|FROM" "$source_file" 2>/dev/null
301	# Flag: static returns with no query
302	grep -n -E "return.*json\(\s*\[\]|return.*json\(\s*\{\}" "$source_file" 2>/dev/null
303	```
304	
305	4. **Check for disconnected props** — props passed to child components that are hardcoded empty at the call site
306	
307	```bash
308	# Find where the component is used and check prop values
309	grep -r -A 3 "<${COMPONENT_NAME}" "${search_path:-src/}" --include="*.tsx" 2>/dev/null | grep -E "=\{(\[\]|\{\}|null|''|\"\")\}"
310	```
311	
312	**Data-flow status:**
313	
314	| Data Source                        | Produces Real Data | Status         |
315	| ---------------------------------- | ------------------ | -------------- |
316	| DB query found                     | Yes                | ✓ FLOWING      |
317	| Fetch exists, static fallback only | No                 | ⚠️ STATIC      |
318	| No data source found               | N/A                | ✗ DISCONNECTED |
319	| Props hardcoded empty at call site | No                 | ✗ HOLLOW_PROP  |
320	
321	**Final Artifact Status (updated with Level 4):**
322	
323	| Exists | Substantive | Wired | Data Flows | Status                                  |
324	| ------ | ----------- | ----- | ---------- | --------------------------------------- |
325	| ✓      | ✓           | ✓     | ✓          | ✓ VERIFIED                              |
326	| ✓      | ✓           | ✓     | ✗          | ⚠️ HOLLOW — wired but data disconnected |
327	| ✓      | ✓           | ✗     | -          | ⚠️ ORPHANED                             |
328	| ✓      | ✗           | -     | -          | ✗ STUB                                  |
329	| ✗      | -           | -     | -          | ✗ MISSING                               |
330	
331	## Step 5: Verify Key Links (Wiring)
332	
333	Key links are critical connections. If broken, the goal fails even with all artifacts present.
334	
335	Use `gsd-tools query` for key link verification against must_haves in PLAN frontmatter:
336	
337	```bash
338	LINKS_RESULT=$(gsd-tools query verify.key-links "$PLAN_PATH")
339	```
340	
341	Parse JSON result: `{ all_verified, verified, total, links: [{from, to, via, verified, detail}] }`
342	
343	For each link:
344	
345	- `verified=true` → WIRED
346	- `verified=false` with "not found" in detail → NOT_WIRED
347	- `verified=false` with "Pattern not found" → PARTIAL
348	
349	**Fallback patterns** (if must_haves.key_links not defined in PLAN):
350	
351	### Pattern: Component → API
352	
353	```bash
354	grep -E "fetch\(['\"].*$api_path|axios\.(get|post).*$api_path" "$component" 2>/dev/null
355	grep -A 5 "fetch\|axios" "$component" | grep -E "await|\.then|setData|setState" 2>/dev/null
356	```
357	
358	Status: WIRED (call + response handling) | PARTIAL (call, no response use) | NOT_WIRED (no call)
359	
360	### Pattern: API → Database
361	
362	```bash
363	grep -E "prisma\.$model|db\.$model|$model\.(find|create|update|delete)" "$route" 2>/dev/null
364	grep -E "return.*json.*\w+|res\.json\(\w+" "$route" 2>/dev/null
365	```
366	
367	Status: WIRED (query + result returned) | PARTIAL (query, static return) | NOT_WIRED (no query)
368	
369	### Pattern: Form → Handler
370	
371	```bash
372	grep -E "onSubmit=\{|handleSubmit" "$component" 2>/dev/null
373	grep -A 10 "onSubmit.*=" "$component" | grep -E "fetch|axios|mutate|dispatch" 2>/dev/null
374	```
375	
376	Status: WIRED (handler + API call) | STUB (only logs/preventDefault) | NOT_WIRED (no handler)
377	
378	### Pattern: State → Render
379	
380	```bash
381	grep -E "useState.*$state_var|\[$state_var," "$component" 2>/dev/null
382	grep -E "\{.*$state_var.*\}|\{$state_var\." "$component" 2>/dev/null
383	```
384	
385	Status: WIRED (state displayed) | NOT_WIRED (state exists, not rendered)

416	## Step 7: Scan for Anti-Patterns
417	
418	Identify files modified in this phase from SUMMARY.md key-files section, or extract commits and verify:
419	
420	```bash
421	# Option 1: Extract from SUMMARY frontmatter
422	SUMMARY_FILES=$(gsd-tools query summary-extract "$PHASE_DIR"/*-SUMMARY.md --fields key-files)
423	
424	# Option 2: Verify commits exist (if commit hashes documented)
425	COMMIT_HASHES=$(grep -oE "[a-f0-9]{7,40}" "$PHASE_DIR"/*-SUMMARY.md | head -10)
426	if [ -n "$COMMIT_HASHES" ]; then

432	```
433	
434	Run anti-pattern detection on each file:
435	
436	```bash
437	# Debt-marker comments
438	grep -n -E "TBD|FIXME|XXX" "$file" 2>/dev/null
439	# Warning-level cleanup comments
440	grep -n -E "TODO|HACK|PLACEHOLDER" "$file" 2>/dev/null
441	grep -n -E "placeholder|coming soon|will be here|not yet implemented|not available" "$file" -i 2>/dev/null
442	# Empty implementations
443	grep -n -E "return null|return \{\}|return \[\]|=> \{\}" "$file" 2>/dev/null
444	# Hardcoded empty data (common stub patterns)
445	grep -n -E "=\s*\[\]|=\s*\{\}|=\s*null|=\s*undefined" "$file" 2>/dev/null | grep -v -E "(test|spec|mock|fixture|\.test\.|\.spec\.)" 2>/dev/null
446	# Props with hardcoded empty values (React/Vue/Svelte stub indicators)
447	grep -n -E "=\{(\[\]|\{\}|null|undefined|''|\"\")\}" "$file" 2>/dev/null
448	# Console.log only implementations
449	grep -n -B 2 -A 2 "console\.log" "$file" 2>/dev/null | grep -E "^\s*(const|function|=>)"
450	```
451	
452	**Stub classification:** A grep match is a STUB only when the value flows to rendering or user-visible output AND no other code path populates it with real data. A test helper, type default, or initial state that gets overwritten by a fetch/store is NOT a stub. Check for data-fetching (useEffect, fetch, query, useSWR, useQuery, subscribe) that writes to the same variable before flagging.
453	
454	**Debt marker gate:** Any `TBD`, `FIXME`, or `XXX` marker in a file modified by this phase is a 🛑 BLOCKER unless the same line references formal follow-up work (`issue #123`, `PR #123`, `#123`, or `DEF-*`). Unreferenced markers mean completion is not auditable; set `status: gaps_found` and list each marker under `gaps`.
455	
456	Categorize: 🛑 Blocker (prevents goal or unresolved debt marker) | ⚠️ Warning (incomplete) | ℹ️ Info (notable)
457	
458	## Step 7b: Behavioral Spot-Checks
459	
460	Anti-pattern scanning (Step 7) checks for code smells. Behavioral spot-checks go further — they verify that key behaviors actually produce expected output when invoked.
461	
462	**When to run:** For phases that produce runnable code (APIs, CLI tools, build scripts, data pipelines). Skip for documentation-only or config-only phases.
463	
464	**How:**
465	
466	1. **Identify checkable behaviors** from must-haves truths. Select 2-4 that can be tested with a single command:
467	
468	```bash
469	# API endpoint returns non-empty data
470	curl -s http://localhost:$PORT/api/$ENDPOINT 2>/dev/null | node -e "let b='';process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');process.stdin.on('data',c=>b+=c);process.stdin.on('end',()=>{const d=JSON.parse(b);process.exit(Array.isArray(d)?(d.length>0?0:1):(Object.keys(d).length>0?0:1))})"
471	
472	# CLI command produces expected output
473	node $CLI_PATH --help 2>&1 | grep -q "$EXPECTED_SUBCOMMAND"
474	
475	# Build produces output files
476	ls $BUILD_OUTPUT_DIR/*.{js,css} 2>/dev/null | wc -l
477	
478	# Module exports expected functions
479	node -e "const m = require('$MODULE_PATH'); console.log(typeof m.$FUNCTION_NAME)" 2>/dev/null | grep -q "function"
480	
481	# A test EXISTS (existence proof — enumerate, do NOT run the suite)
482	cargo test -- --list 2>/dev/null | grep -q "$PHASE_TEST_PATTERN"   # pytest --collect-only -q · npx vitest list · go test -list '.*'
483	
484	# A specific test PASSES (run ONE named test, never the whole suite)
485	cargo test "$TEST_NAME" -- --exact   # pytest -k "$TEST_NAME" · npx vitest run -t "$TEST_NAME"
486	```
487	
488	2. **Run each check** and record pass/fail:
489	
490	**Spot-check status:**
491	
492	| Behavior | Command   | Result   | Status                   |
493	| -------- | --------- | -------- | ------------------------ |
494	| {truth}  | {command} | {output} | ✓ PASS / ✗ FAIL / ? SKIP |
495	
496	3. **Classification:**
497	   - ✓ PASS: Command succeeded and output matches expected
498	   - ✗ FAIL: Command failed or output is empty/wrong — flag as gap
499	   - ? SKIP: Can't test without running server/external service — route to human verification (Step 8)
500	
501	**Spot-check constraints:**
502	
503	- Each check must complete in under 10 seconds
504	- Do not start servers or services — only test what's already runnable
505	- Do not modify state (no writes, no mutations, no side effects)
506	- **Run the full workspace test command at most once per verification.** Never filter a full run per must-have (`<full-suite> 2>&1 | grep X` repeated per truth) — it re-runs everything and yields no new evidence. Prove a test exists by enumeration (`--list` / `--collect-only`); prove one passes via a single named test. If a full run is genuinely required, run it once and `grep` the saved output.
507	- If the project has no runnable entry points yet, skip with: "Step 7b: SKIPPED (no runnable entry points)"
508	
509	## Step 7c: Probe Execution
510	
511	SUMMARY.md probe pass claims are not evidence. If a phase declares or implies probe-based verification, the verifier must run the probe in its own process and record the command result.
512	
513	**When to run:** For migration phases, CLI/tooling phases, or any phase whose PLAN/SUMMARY/verification criteria mention probes, PASS markers, stage markers, runnable checks, or `scripts/*/tests/probe-*.sh`.
514	
515	**Probe discovery:**
516	
517	```bash
518	# Conventional project probes
519	find scripts -path '*/tests/probe-*.sh' -type f 2>/dev/null | sort
520	
521	# Phase-declared probes
522	grep -R -n -E 'probe-[^[:space:]]+\.sh|scripts/.*/tests/probe-.*\.sh' "$PHASE_DIR"/*-PLAN.md "$PHASE_DIR"/*-SUMMARY.md 2>/dev/null
523	```
524	
525	**Execution contract:**
526	
527	1. Build the `PROBES` list from explicit PLAN declarations first; include conventional `scripts/*/tests/probe-*.sh` when the phase is a migration/tooling phase or the success criteria mention probes.
528	2. For every documented probe path, if the file is missing or unreadable, mark `MISSING_PROBE` and set `status: gaps_found`. Do not require the executable bit because probes run through `bash "$probe"`.
529	3. Run each probe from the built `PROBES` list (declared + conventional) from the repository root:
530	
531	```bash
532	for probe in "${PROBES[@]}"; do
533	  timeout 30s bash "$probe"
534	done
535	```
536	
537	4. Exit code 0 is PASS. Any non-zero exit is FAILED and must include stdout/stderr evidence in VERIFICATION.md.
538	5. Do not substitute executor narration, SUMMARY.md PASS-marker counts, or a different dry-run driver command for the probe result.
539	
540	**Probe status:**
541	
542	| Probe                       | Command         | Result           | Status                        |
543	| --------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------- |
544	| `scripts/.../probe-name.sh` | `bash "$probe"` | exit code/output | PASS / FAILED / MISSING_PROBE |
545	
546	## Step 8: Identify Human Verification Needs
547	
548	**Always needs human:** Visual appearance, user flow completion, real-time behavior, external service integration, performance feel, error message clarity.
549	
550	**Needs human if uncertain:** Complex wiring grep can't trace, dynamic state behavior, edge cases.
551	
552	**Harvest deferred items from PLAN.md (#3309 / `workflow.human_verify_mode = end-of-phase`):** Scan every PLAN file in the phase for `<verify><human-check>` blocks on `auto` tasks. These are verification items the planner deliberately deferred from `checkpoint:human-verify` to end-of-phase to avoid the executor cold-start cost. Each block has the same shape used by the planner:
553	
554	```xml
555	<verify>
556	  <human-check>
557	    <test>What to do</test>
558	    <expected>What should happen</expected>
559	    <why_human>Why grep can't verify</why_human>
560	  </human-check>
561	</verify>
562	```
563	
564	Merge those harvested items into the same human verification list as your own analysis. Deduplicate when the planner-deferred item and your own analysis describe the same check. The downstream `human_needed` → `{phase_num}-UAT.md` path in `workflows/execute-phase.md` is the single sink — no separate file is created.
565	
566	**Format:**
567	
568	```markdown
569	### 1. {Test Name}
570	
571	**Test:** {What to do}
572	**Expected:** {What should happen}
573	**Why human:** {Why can't verify programmatically}
574	```
575	
576	## Step 9: Determine Overall Status
577	
578	Classify status using this decision tree IN ORDER (most restrictive first):
579	
580	1. IF any truth FAILED, artifact MISSING/STUB, key link NOT_WIRED, or blocker anti-pattern found:
581	   → **status: gaps_found**
582	
583	2. IF Step 8 produced ANY human verification items (section is non-empty):
584	   → **status: human_needed**
585	   (Even if all truths are VERIFIED and score is N/N — human items take priority)
586	
587	3. IF all truths VERIFIED, all artifacts pass, all links WIRED, no blockers, AND no human verification items:
588	   → **status: passed**
589	
590	**passed is ONLY valid when the human verification section is empty.** If you identified items requiring human testing in Step 8, status MUST be human_needed.
591	
592	> **Shared status seam**: the status vocabulary (`passed`, `gaps_found`, `human_needed`) and the per-status routing (next action and next command for each value) are owned by `src/verification.cts` via `gsd_run query verification.status`. This agent is the single emitter of the frontmatter status field; consumers (ship.md, execute-phase.md) read routing from that query instead of re-deriving it.
593	
594	**Score:** `verified_truths / total_truths`
595	
596	## Step 9b: Filter Deferred Items
597	
598	Before reporting gaps, check if any identified gaps are explicitly addressed in later phases of the current milestone. This prevents false-positive gap reports for items intentionally scheduled for future work.
599	
600	**Load the full milestone roadmap:**
601	
602	```bash
603	ROADMAP_DATA=$(gsd-tools query roadmap.analyze --raw)
604	```
605	
606	Parse the JSON to extract all phases. Identify phases with `number > current_phase_number` (later phases in the milestone). For each later phase, extract its `goal` and `success_criteria`.
607	
608	**For each potential gap identified in Step 9:**
609	
610	1. Check if the gap's failed truth or missing item is covered by a later phase's goal or success criteria
611	2. **Match criteria:** The gap's concern appears in a later phase's goal text, success criteria text, or the later phase's name clearly suggests it covers this area of work
612	3. If a match is found → move the gap to the `deferred` list, recording which phase addresses it and the matching evidence (goal text or success criterion)
613	4. If the gap does not match any later phase → keep it as a real `gap`
614	
615	**Important:** Be conservative when matching. Only defer a gap when there is clear, specific evidence in a later phase's roadmap section. Vague or tangential matches should NOT cause a gap to be deferred — when in doubt, keep it as a real gap.
616	
617	**Deferred items do NOT affect the status determination.** After filtering, recalculate:
618	
619	- If the gaps list is now empty and no human verification items exist → `passed`
620	- If the gaps list is now empty but human verification items exist → `human_needed`
621	- If the gaps list still has items → `gaps_found`
622	
623	## Step 10: Structure Gap Output (If Gaps Found)
624	
625	Before writing VERIFICATION.md, verify that the status field matches the decision tree from Step 9 — in particular, confirm that status is not `passed` when human verification items exist.
626	
627	Structure gaps in YAML frontmatter for `/gsd-plan-phase --gaps`:
628	
629	```yaml
630	gaps:
631	  - truth: 'Observable truth that failed'
632	    status: failed
633	    reason: 'Brief explanation'
634	    artifacts:
635	      - path: 'src/path/to/file.tsx'
636	        issue: "What's wrong"
637	    missing:
638	      - 'Specific thing to add/fix'
639	```
640	
641	- `truth`: The observable truth that failed
642	- `status`: failed | partial
643	- `reason`: Brief explanation
644	- `artifacts`: Files with issues
645	- `missing`: Specific things to add/fix
646	
647	If Step 9b identified deferred items, add a `deferred` section after `gaps`:
648	
649	```yaml
650	deferred: # Items addressed in later phases — not actionable gaps
651	  - truth: 'Observable truth not yet met'
652	    addressed_in: 'Phase 5'
653	    evidence: "Phase 5 success criteria: 'Implement RuntimeConfigC FFI bindings'"
654	```
655	
656	Deferred items are informational only — they do not require closure plans.
657	
658	**Group related gaps by concern** — if multiple truths fail from the same root cause, note this to help the planner create focused plans.
659	
660	</verification_process>
661	
662	<mvp_mode_verification>
663	
664	## MVP Mode Verification
665	
666	**When the phase under verification has `mode: mvp` in ROADMAP.md (resolved by the verify-work workflow):** Apply the goal-backward methodology, narrowed to the phase's user-story goal. Required reading: `@/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.claude/gsd-core/references/verify-mvp-mode.md`.
667	
668	**Core narrowing rule:** Goal-backward verification normally checks that the phase goal is observably true in the codebase. Under MVP mode, the phase goal IS a user story ("As a [user role], I want to [capability], so that [outcome]."). Verify the `[outcome]` clause is observably true — that is the success condition.
669	
670	**VERIFICATION.md output structure under MVP mode:**
671	
672	1. Top-level "User Flow Coverage" table: each step of the user story → expected → evidence in codebase → status. (Format defined in `references/verify-mvp-mode.md`.)
673	2. Standard technical-check sections (API verification, error handling, etc.) follow below — only if the user flow coverage is complete.
674	
675	**User Story format guard:** Apply via the centralized verb instead of inlining the regex:
676	
677	```bash
678	USER_STORY_VALID=$(gsd-tools query user-story.validate --story "$PHASE_GOAL" --pick valid)
679	```
680	
681	If `valid != true`, refuse to verify. Surface the discrepancy and ask the user to run `/gsd mvp-phase ${PHASE}` to set a proper User Story goal. The verb owns the canonical regex `/^As a .+, I want to .+, so that .+\.$/` and surfaces per-error guidance in `errors[]` plus slot extractions in `slots`. Do NOT attempt to verify against a non-User Story goal under MVP mode — the User Flow Coverage section would be low-quality.
682	
683	**Mode is all-or-nothing per phase** (PRD decision Q1, inherited from Phase 1). The MVP Mode Verification rules apply to the whole phase or not at all.
684	
685	**Compatibility with existing verifier behavior:** When the phase mode is null/absent, this section is dormant. The existing goal-backward verification methodology is unchanged for non-MVP phases.
686	
687	</mvp_mode_verification>

854	
855	**DO NOT trust SUMMARY claims.** Verify the component actually renders messages, not a placeholder.
856	
857	**DO NOT assume existence = implementation.** Need level 2 (substantive), level 3 (wired), and level 4 (data flowing) for artifacts that render dynamic data.

903	
904	```typescript
905	// Fetch exists but response ignored:
906	fetch('/api/messages')  // No await, no .then, no assignment
907	
908	// Query exists but result not returned:

910	return Response.json({ ok: true })  // Returns static, not query result

912	// Handler only prevents default:

914	
915	// State exists but not rendered:
916	const [messages, setMessages] = useState([])
917	return <div>No messages</div>  // Always shows "no messages"

```
1	# Requirements: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
2	
3	**Defined:** 2026-03-07
4	**Core Value:** Zero-knowledge privacy -- files encrypted client-side, server never sees plaintext
5	
6	## v1.1 Requirements
7	
8	Requirements for IPFS infrastructure milestone. Each maps to roadmap phases.
9	
10	### IPNS Reliability
11	
12	- [x] **IPNS-01**: Self-hosted Someguy deployed alongside Kubo, replacing delegated-ipfs.dev as primary IPNS routing provider
13	- [x] **IPNS-02**: IPNS resolution uses DB-first strategy with async Kubo DHT verification via self-hosted Someguy
14	- [x] **IPNS-03**: Recovery tool resolves IPNS via self-hosted Someguy instead of delegated-ipfs.dev
15	- [x] **IPNS-04**: System degrades gracefully when DHT resolution is slow (timeout + DB fallback within 2s)
16	
17	### Vault Migration
18	
19	- [x] **VAULT-01**: rootFolderKey embedded in IPFS vault blob v2 format (ECIES-wrapped in blob header)
20	- [x] **VAULT-02**: Client reads rootFolderKey exclusively from IPFS v2 blob on login (DB crypto columns dropped)
21	- [x] **VAULT-03**: All vaults migrated to v2 — dead migration code removed, DB columns dropped
22	- [x] **VAULT-04**: encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey column dropped from vaults table (HKDF-derivable)
23	- [x] **VAULT-05**: Recovery tool updated to parse vault blob v2 format

110	### IPNS Enhancements
111

118	- **DB-02**: pinned_cids table eliminated (alternative quota tracking via IPFS MFS or client-reported)
119	
120	### BYO-IPFS Advanced
121	
122	- **BYO-08**: Client-direct IPFS upload mode (bypass server relay for power users)
123	
124	## Out of Scope
125	
126	Explicitly excluded. Documented to prevent scope creep.
127	
128	| Feature                             | Reason                                                                                                                                |
129	| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
130	| Full database elimination (zero DB) | Auth tables (users, auth_methods, refresh_tokens) require indexed queryable storage. IPFS is not a database.                          |
131	| IPNS PubSub as primary resolution   | Only works when publisher and resolver share PubSub peers. Not persistent. Doesn't scale to thousands of IPNS names per user.         |
132	| CRDT for all metadata               | Premature -- optimistic concurrency already solves folder conflicts. CRDTs add state size growth and cross-platform merge complexity. |
133	| DNSLink as IPNS alternative         | Requires DNS infrastructure per user. Propagation is slow. Doesn't support per-folder/per-file IPNS model.                            |
134	| Share migration to IPFS             | Requires CRDT inbox protocol (research-only this milestone). Complex query patterns (filter by recipient, status, revocation).        |
135	| Encrypted Productivity Suite        | Deferred to Milestone 4 (v2.0) -- billing, teams, doc editors, signing, AWS Nitro TEE                                                 |
136	| Mobile apps                         | Deferred to Milestone 4+                                                                                                              |
137
```

```
### Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation

**Goal**: Operators can observe IPFS/IPNS latency and API performance in Prometheus/Grafana before any architectural changes are made
**Depends on**: Nothing (first phase of milestone)
**Requirements**: PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-03, PERF-04
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

1. Prometheus exposes duration histograms for IPNS resolve, IPNS publish, IPFS pin, and IPFS cat operations with per-operation labels
2. API endpoint response times are captured at p50/p95/p99 for all critical routes (auth, IPNS resolve/publish, file upload/download, folder CRUD)
3. Kubo node health metrics (peer count, bandwidth, datastore size) are visible in Prometheus via scraped Kubo endpoint
4. TEE republish batch duration histogram captures per-batch timing with success/failure labels

**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete

Plans:

- [x] 18-01-PLAN.md -- Add IPFS/IPNS + TEE duration histograms to MetricsService and instrument service/controller timing
- [x] 18-02-PLAN.md -- Add Kubo scrape to Alloy, extend Grafana dashboard, create baseline benchmark script======== 31 ========
### Phase 31: Structural Decomposition

**Goal**: Monolithic files exceeding 900 lines are split into focused, testable modules without breaking existing functionality
**Depends on**: Phase 28 (logger available for decomposed modules)
**Requirements**: None (maintainability improvement)
**Research flag**: NEEDS `/gsd:discuss-phase` -- decomposition boundaries for useSharedNavigation (navigation vs key management vs write ops), container/presentational split for FileBrowser/SharedFileBrowser, alignment with SDK extraction direction for folder.service.ts
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

1. `useSharedNavigation.ts` (1199 lines) is split into 3+ focused hooks (navigation state, key unwrapping, write operations) with each under 400 lines
2. `FileBrowser.tsx` (964 lines) and `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` (943 lines) are split into container + presentational components
3. `folder.service.ts` (1089 lines) is decomposed into focused modules (CRUD, metadata, publish coordination)
4. All existing E2E tests pass after decomposition (sharing-workflow, writable-shares, full-workflow)
5. No new `any` casts or type regressions introduced

**Plans**: 3/3 plans executed

Plans:

- [x] 31-01-PLAN.md -- SDK-side module extraction (tree utils, error utils, share context)
- [x] 31-02-PLAN.md -- Web layer barrel re-exports and SDK adoption
- [x] 31-03-PLAN.md -- Hook split and component extraction======== 32 ========
### Phase 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution

**Goal**: FUSE FilePointer resolution no longer blocks the filesystem thread, eliminating Finder "connection lost" errors during metadata refresh
**Depends on**: None (can run in parallel with other phases)
**Requirements**: None (performance improvement)
**Research flag**: Skip -- channel-based async pattern is well-defined, contained to crates/fuse
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

1. FilePointer resolution spawns async tasks via a channel pair instead of blocking the FUSE callback thread
2. Finder operations (ls, open, copy) do not stall or disconnect during background metadata refresh
3. Resolution latency is bounded by a timeout rather than O(N \* network_timeout)
4. Desktop E2E tests pass with the async resolution path

**Plans:** 3/3 plans complete

Plans:

- [x] 32-01-PLAN.md -- Add PendingFilePointer channel infrastructure to CipherBoxFS (enum, channel pair, dedup guard, drain method)
- [x] 32-02-PLAN.md -- Refactor drain_refresh_completions to spawn async FilePointer resolution (replace block_with_timeout with rt.spawn)
- [x] 32-03-PLAN.md -- Handle open/read for unresolved FilePointers with poll-wait fallback (5s poll timeout, EIO on miss)
```

```
98	#### 1. Kubo peer count and bandwidth visibility on staging
99	
100	**Test:** Open the Grafana "Kubo Health" row against live staging and query `libp2p_swarm_connections_opened_total`, `libp2p_network_in_bytes_total`, `libp2p_network_out_bytes_total` in Prometheus.

Only a live Kubo node (with metrics enabled or a newer Kubo) can confirm this. Not derivable from the codebase.

104	#### 2. Kubo-sourced datastore size metric
105	
106	**Test:** Query Prometheus for a datastore/repo-size metric scraped from Kubo's `/debug/metrics/prometheus` endpoint.

Confirming whether Kubo exposes a usable datastore metric requires a live node.
109	
110	### Gaps Summary
111	
112	The API-side instrumentation contract (PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-04) is fully achieved and wired in current code: two new histograms are registered on the Prometheus registry with correct labels/buckets, all four IPFS/IPNS operations (resolve, publish, pin, cat) and the TEE republish batch are instrumented with start/end timers carrying success/failure result labels, the HTTP interceptor is globally registered, and the Grafana dashboard renders p50/p95/p99 via `histogram_quantile`. The baselines document and benchmark script exist with real per-operation and per-route p50/p95/p99 values from staging.
113	
114	The one open item is PERF-03's runtime visibility: the Kubo Alloy scrape block and Grafana Kubo Health panels are present and wired, but the project's own staging baseline documents that Kubo v0.34.0 does not emit libp2p peer/bandwidth metrics (panels show "No data") and there is no datastore-size panel sourced from the Kubo endpoint. This is a live-infrastructure observation that cannot be resolved from the codebase, so it is routed to human verification rather than marked a code blocker — the scrape wiring itself is correct and would surface data on a Kubo version that exposes those metrics. No code blockers were found.
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/feedback-infra-limited-items-not-human-verify.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-infra-limited-items-not-human-verify\ndescription: Don't bucket unmet criteria caused by upstream/infra limits as \"human verification\" — record as an accepted override\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nWhen a phase success criterion or requirement can't be satisfied because of an upstream/infrastructure limitation (not a code defect), do NOT route it to \"human verification\" — no human action can change it. Record it as an accepted override / known limitation in the VERIFICATION.md frontmatter (gsd `overrides:` block) and mark the phase `passed`.\n\n**Why:** During the v1.1 verification-ledger close-out (phases 18/31/32, 2026-06-19), the gsd-verifier marked PERF-03 `human_needed` for Kubo node-health metrics. The user pushed back — \"i dont think human will help with missing data in kubo\" — Kubo v0.34 emits no libp2p peer/bandwidth metrics upstream, so a human check would never resolve. The deliverable (Alloy scrape + dashboard panels) was correctly wired; the gap is upstream.\n\n**How to apply:** Classify unmet items by *actionability*, not just verifiability. Code defect → gap. Genuinely runtime-only and confirmable (live Finder/E2E/UAT) → human_needed/UAT. Upstream/infra constraint with the deliverable correctly wired → accepted override (`reason` + `accepted_by` + `accepted_at`), status `passed`. See [[reference-kubo-v034-no-libp2p-metrics]].\n"
}
```

The scrape will populate automatically on a Kubo build that exposes these metrics — revisit on Kubo upgrade before treating PERF-03 panels as broken. 3	## Markdown Lint Rules (pre-commit hook)
```
4	
5	The repo enforces `markdownlint` via lint-staged on commit. Common violations:
6	
7	- **MD036**: Don't use `**bold text**` as a heading — use proper `###` headings instead
8	- **MD031/MD032**: Blank lines required around fenced code blocks and lists
9	- Italic footers like `*Last updated: ...*` trigger MD036 — use plain text instead
10	- **`.planning/` is excluded from markdownlint** (lint-staged runs `markdownlint --fix --ignore .planning`) — it's excluded on purpose; do NOT manually run markdownlint on files under `.planning/` (todos, reports, phases). Prettier still runs on them.
```

```
42	## gh CLI Auth
43	
44	- [gh requires env -u GITHUB_TOKEN](feedback_gh_auth.md) — always prefix gh commands with `env -u GITHUB_TOKEN`
45	- [PR-create triggers a bot release commit](project-gh-pr-create-bot-push.md) — after `gh pr create`, github-actions[bot] pushes `chore(release)` to the branch; the next local push is rejected until `git fetch` + `git rebase origin/<branch>`
46	- [gh pr edit fails on Projects-classic](project-gh-pr-edit-projects-classic-fails.md) — `gh pr edit` exits 1 (`projectCards` GraphQL deprecation) on this repo; patch PR body/title via `gh api -X PATCH repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/<N> -F body=@file`. Also: wrap bare `#NN` scope-item refs in backticks in PR bodies so GFM doesn't auto-link them as issues
47	
48	## CodeRabbit CLI
49	
50	- [Use --agent not --plain](feedback-coderabbit-cli-use-agent-flag.md) — `coderabbit review --agent` gives structured findings that are easier to parse than `--plain`; keep `--base-commit <sha>` to scope a single phase's diff
51	- [GitHub review nitpicks live in the review body, not threads](project-coderabbit-github-review-body-not-threads.md) — post-PR CodeRabbit bundles nitpicks in the COMMENTED review body; `/resolve-pr-reviews` thread query returns 0; parse `pulls/N/reviews[].body` and verify suggestions vs live code
```
